# Device-view HTML sink worksheet

## Method and scope

This audit follows plan 023. Candidate inventory includes `innerHTML`, `QH`, `QA`, and `format` calls, including reads and commented candidates. Each view section records source-to-sink classifications and exclusions. Comma-separated line numbers identify equivalent flows; line references use the edited view. Multiple source values at one sink require separate classifications. A classification is not evidence that unrelated interpolations on the same line are safe.

Initial exact `grep -n 'innerHTML\|QH(' ... | wc -l` results were 245 for default, 261 for default3, and 100 for mobile. The full four-form initial inventory contained 992 candidate lines. The initial prerequisite stop was cleared after plan 016 was integrated at `ff19215ae9e3a41e7c14e9b23529ceb4543e4413`.

The reviewer authorized these context-specific fixes beyond the original plan's JavaScript/URL stop conditions:

- Plain text: `EscapeHtml` at an HTML boundary, or `textContent`/input `value` destinations.
- Inline JavaScript string arguments: existing `encodeURIComponentEx`, with matching consumer decoding and preserved legacy callers.
- External links: allow links only when the trimmed value matches `/^https?:\/\//i`; otherwise show escaped text without `href`.

Desktop `format` escapes substitution arguments, not its constant template. Mobile `format` escapes the complete formatted result. Neither contract justifies adding an extra escaping layer to an already escaped value.

`yes` identifies an escaping or text-only boundary. `n/a` requires precise producer/consumer evidence for constants, numeric data, or non-HTML operations. The inventory test checks accounting and column values; it is not a taint analyzer. Regex tests protect only named regression shapes.

## Manual verification

Browser check: **NOT RUN**. No authorized test-server/browser check was performed. Static tests and syntax checks do not establish browser-confirmed safety.

Server-side plugin URL validation remains outside this view-only change. Client-side scheme allowlisting is not a replacement for server-side validation in other consumers.

## views/default.handlebars

Final source inventory: **402 candidate lines**, matched with `innerHTML|QH\s*\(|QA\s*\(|format\s*\(`. The worksheet contains **1097 source/exclusion records**: **882 yes**, **215 n/a**, and **235 records documenting applied boundary remediation**. There are no unremediated or unknown rows. Record counts are not independent vulnerabilities: one shared fix can protect several sinks, and defensive key encoding is explicitly identified. Numeric-boundary remediations can also be n/a exclusions from string injection.

All line numbers refer to the edited view. Additional rows include source callers and non-HTML property boundaries outside the candidate regex, so each indirect dialog flow and each changed handler remains documented. The generic dialog forwarding rows do not assert that arbitrary caller HTML is safe.

Boundary definitions: `yes` means the named source is escaped, encoded for its JS context, sanitized through the existing supported sanitizer, or written through a non-HTML property, as detailed. `n/a` means the row is a non-sink, constant, bounded numeric derivation, non-rendered value, or a source that cannot contribute attacker text. `format` at21804 escapes every replacement with `EscapeHtml`; its constant markup template is intentionally preserved. Existing nested escaping remains unchanged. `EscapeHtml` at public/scripts/common-0.0.1.js38 returns undefined for objects rather than implicitly serializing them.

Provenance cautions: full identifiers are not assumed safe merely because hash suffixes are constrained; domain prefixes are included in encoding. `urlargs` comes from `parseUriArgs()` at1748, not the unused `getUrlVars`;1749-1750 string-coerce and reject non-alphanumeric `key` values using common114. Added key encoders are defensive. Fixed-purpose `otpauth:`, `mcrouter:` and existing application-protocol links are distinguished from arbitrary plugin/share URLs; arbitrary rendered links require a trimmed HTTP(S) scheme and otherwise render escaped text.

### NOTES

- Custom UI dialog titles/text and other server text now render literally where executable markup previously passed through. No new sanitizer was added and no complete trusted HTML fragment was escaped.
- Existing DOMPurify3.4.5 can return dirty input when unsupported (public/scripts/purify.js1430-1432). Both notes paths now require `DOMPurify.isSupported`; their existing empty/escaped-text fallback is retained.
- JavaScript handlers use `encodeURIComponentEx` with decoding at the existing consumer or inline before a raw consumer. `goPlugin` additionally accepts an encoded flag so direct raw callers keep their contract. `gotoUser` already decodes, so report links do not decode twice. Context-menu extraction was adjusted to decode the encoded IDs from handler text.
- Plugin homepage/changelog allowlisting protects rendered links only. Server-side plugin download/installation URL validation was not changed; server/plugin policy remains a separate limitation.
- This is a static source-to-sink worksheet, not a claim of runtime or browser verification. No builds, tests, formatters, commits, or runtime security probes ran. Existing unrelated UI defects and pre-existing double escaping were not expanded into cleanup.

## views/default3.handlebars

Candidate inventory: **418 final candidate lines** matching `innerHTML|QH\s*\(|QA\s*\(|format\s*\(`. The starting inventory had 423 lines; five HTML writes became text/value assignments. The table also records these removed sinks and additional leaf boundaries.

Classification: **1307 source-to-sink rows**, including **303 remediated flow rows** and **200 exclusions**. Counts are table rows, not unique vulnerabilities: one changed scalar can reach multiple sinks or contexts. `yes` describes the final boundary; remediation identifies changes. `n/a` identifies reads, comments, fixed markup, unreachable branches, or proven numeric/literal output. No row treats a complete generated HTML fragment as plain text.

Evidence conventions: line numbers in the first column refer to this final view. Explicit producer paths refer to read-only server/helper sources. QH/QA and addHtmlValue/addDeviceAttribute preserve HTML. Local format escapes each substitution with EscapeHtml; its templates and surrounding concatenations are classified separately. printDate/printTime/printDateTime/printTimer escape their output. Encoded IDs remain encoded only while crossing JS/URL boundaries; existing raw-ID consumers receive decoded strings. Device IDs include a domain segment whose charset is not guaranteed merely by the generated hash suffix.

### Change and regression notes

- Text fixes cover warning/status/error messages, plain notes, custom UI names, network/interface values, OTP recovery values, user phone/realms, report fallback cells, statistics keys/values, file names, software errors, and modal leaf values.
- Attribute fixes preserve raw DOM values using quoted EscapeHtml or input.value. This includes node/mesh selector IDs, map placement IDs, file-upload cookies, custom UI IDs, and permission IDs.
- JS strings use encodeURIComponentEx with existing or inline decodeURIComponent. Store app.name and desktop app.uninstall retain their existing decoder. Plugin goPlugin accepts the optional encoded flag and preserves legacy raw callers; both shortName and title are decoded only when flagged. Plugin title uses textContent and its iframe query pin is encoded.
- Context-menu introspection decodes extracted encoded IDs at lines 7970, 8030, and 8069. Exercise lookup and action dispatch with quotes, whitespace, percent signs, and domain-containing full IDs.
- Full untrusted plugin/shared-link/Google Drive/Discord/ntfy hrefs require a trimmed http/https prefix; rejected values remain escaped text with no href. Plugin homepage expression is at23555; changelog expressions at23533 and23537. Existing generated otpauth:// and mcrouter:// links retain their protocol contracts and escape the quoted href.
- Detector regressions should exercise single-quoted inline app names, both plugin JS arguments, encoded and legacy goPlugin calls, script-scheme URL rejection, whitespace-trimmed HTTPS URLs, unknown report protocol/records text, statistics keys and values, permission-key IDs, map node IDs, and all four device/group selector option boundaries10536/16793/17210/18038. Preserve literal report node markup and raw selector values.
- Nontrivial boundary checks belong in the parent test file. No tests, builds, formatters, or commits ran in this worker. Only views/default3.handlebars changed in the repository.
- Server plugin metadata validation was inspected but not changed. This view-level scheme allowlist does not add server-side validation for plugin metadata or other consumers; that remains a follow-up limitation, not a safety claim about pluginHandler.

### URL login-key qualification

All `urlargs.key` encoding remediations in this worksheet are defensive boundary hardening, not newly demonstrated exploitable flows. Startup at views/default3.handlebars:2474 coerces a non-null key to a string, and2475 deletes truthy keys rejected by isAlphaNumeric. public/scripts/common-0.0.1.js:114 validates string input with `/^[A-Za-z0-9]+$/`; the numeric shortcut cannot bypass the startup string conversion. Empty keys are omitted by the rendering ternaries. The existing source validation therefore already restricts rendered key characters. Counts remain remediated flow rows, not vulnerability counts.

## views/default-mobile.handlebars

### Inventory and method

- Final full candidate inventory: **157 lines** matching `innerHTML`, `QH\s*\(`, `QA\s*\(`, or `format\s*\(`. Initial inventory had 160 lines; moving username, notes, and current folder to text/value setters removed three candidates without shifting line numbers.
- Final legacy `innerHTML\|QH(` inventory: **97 lines**.
- All positions below are post-edit. Each row records one source flow or a specifically explained exclusion; grouped line lists represent identical boundaries, not blanket approval of every value on a line.
- Shared `public/scripts/common-0.0.1.js:17-18` establishes QA/QH HTML interpretation; `:38` defines EscapeHtml. Local `encodeURIComponentEx` at 8414 additionally encodes apostrophes. The mobile `format` implementation at 8400 escapes the **whole formatted output**, unlike an interpolation-only formatter. This existing contract remains unchanged, including literal markup becoming text and pre-existing double-escaping where callers already escape arguments/results.
- `addHtmlValue`, `addHtmlValue2`, `addHtmlValue4`, `addDeviceAttribute`, and `addDetailItem` preserve supplied fragments (8385-8387,4545-4546,8412). Their dynamic arguments are classified below; complete fragments are not escaped as a fix.
- JS-context fixes use the explicitly authorized encodeURIComponentEx/decodeURIComponent pattern. Most ID handlers decode inline, preserving every existing consumer signature. The public-link helper has one caller; its caller now encodes, and its consumer decodes into input.value.
- Named source classes are present: device name/rname/host/desc, user name/realname/email, mesh name/desc, notes, session messages, console messages, notification text, file names and paths. The events dialog uses `message.events[].m`, `.a`, and `.t` (1906-1911); there is no `events[].msg` access. Chat references are a button, permissions, and the fixed confirmation at 4369; this view does not render a chat transcript.
- No tests, build, formatter, browser check, or commit ran in this worker. Counts are source-inventory measurements, not security-test results. Main owns automated and browser gates.

Classification totals: **323 source-flow records** (384 expanded line/source pairs), **42 fixed flow records** (44 expanded pairs), **72 exclusion records** (102 expanded candidate/exclusion pairs). Six source-flow records use numeric-conversion `n/a` with explicit evidence. Records may share candidate lines because each interpolated field is classified separately.

### Concrete regression shapes and retained limits

Known-bad shapes fixed in this view:

- `QH('p3userName', userinfo.name)`; notes decoded directly into QH; current-folder aliases passed directly to QH.
- Raw `f.nx` inside the file-dialog checkbox value; raw selected filename inside the rename input value.
- Raw `publicPath + '/' + f.nx` in the public-link onclick, followed by raw URL inside the public-link input value.
- Raw node/mesh identifiers inside list/detail onclick handlers and device-list id attributes.
- Raw `message.secret` in the OTP attribute/display, raw backup token halves, `userinfo.phone`, language option labels/values, and display selector keys/labels.
- Raw `node.icon` in quoted classes; raw server-file `f.t` in unquoted class tokens; quota progress attributes and remote file size in JS now explicitly numeric.
- Raw authcookie hidden attributes, URL query key in the APK link, and combined Android pairing href attributes.
- Unescaped keyboard helper return values at their HTML consumers.

Suggested extracted behavior assertions for main:

1. Extract `p5showPublicLink`, stub `setDialogMode` and `Q`, and call with `encodeURIComponentEx` of a URL containing `<`, `>`, ampersands, both quotes, apostrophe, spaces and percent signs. Assert constant input markup contains no payload and `.value` equals the original URL exactly.
2. Exercise the OTP grouping statements at 1844-1847 for ordinary, 32-character and 52-character secret values. Assert escaped display pieces, the single intentional `<br/>` for the 32-character layout, and separately escaped attribute value.
3. Assert `p5getQuotabar` emits only Number-converted attribute values; a hostile size/quota string must not become an attribute.
4. Keep mobile `format` expectations distinct: its entire result is escaped. Do not assert that a `<b>` template remains active markup in this variant.

The Android pairing scheme is not an arbitrary server URL: `meshuser.js:618-625` constructs the literal `mc://` prefix. The fix preserves that application protocol and escapes its quoted attribute; applying an HTTP-only policy here would break pairing. The OTP URL is currently displayed as escaped text because of the existing mobile formatter contract. Agent mailto links require the literal `mailto:` prefix after escaping (4039-4040). There are no plugin homepage/changelog href builders in this view; no server plugin validation was changed.

Existing double-escape presentation behavior is recorded rather than silently claimed correct. Browser checks remain necessary for layout and encoded handler roundtrips. Static source classification does not prove behavior of other templates or arbitrary future helper callers.

## Integration verification

The per-view statements about not running checks describe the slice workers. The integration executor ran the following checks after all view edits and worksheet fragments landed.

- `node --test --test-name-pattern='worksheet inventory' test/xss-sweep.test.js`: exit 0; one test passed. Every final candidate line has an explicit classification or exclusion.
- `node --test --test-name-pattern='worksheet' test/xss-sweep.test.js`: exit 0; two tests passed. There are zero `no` or unknown classifications, and every classification has evidence.
- `node --test test/xss-sweep.test.js`: final exit 0; all 14 tests passed, including detector self-tests, zero known-bad matches across the three real templates, and extracted behavior assertions.
- The first full focused run exposed two test-fixture differences between desktop variants: the alternate navigation helper uses `Q`, and the default homepage expression is inline. The test fixtures were corrected to exercise both actual implementations; the next focused run passed.
- `npm run check && npm test`: exit 0. Syntax baseline checked 75 JavaScript files; the full suite passed 130 tests with zero failures. The syntax baseline does not parse embedded template scripts.
- `git diff --name-only` listed only the three scoped views. `git status --short` additionally listed the two new scoped audit/test files and the pre-provided `node_modules` symlink. The symlink was not changed or staged.
- Browser check: **NOT RUN** as requested. No browser/server validation is claimed.

Final candidate-line counts: default 402, default3 418, mobile 157; total **977**. The original four-form inventory had 992 candidates. Text/value replacements removed HTML candidates; removed flows remain documented.

Final worksheet counts: default 1,097 records (882 `yes`, 215 `n/a`, 235 remediated); default3 1,307 records (1,107 `yes`, 200 `n/a`, 303 remediated); mobile 395 records (317 `yes`, 78 `n/a`, 42 remediated). Total **2,799 records**, **2,306 escaped/text-safe records**, **493 justified `n/a` records**, and **580 remediated records**. Remediation overlaps final safety classifications; these are not disjoint totals or independent vulnerability counts. The `n/a` category includes non-sinks, constants, and proven numeric boundaries, not blanket trust in server data.

Classification and remediation were integrated per view, followed by the prescribed inventory, worksheet, focused, and integration gates. `plans/README.md` was intentionally not updated under reviewer instructions. The worktree has no `plans/` directory, so this committed worksheet contains execution and manual-check evidence instead of modifying the external plan.

Residual limitations: browser layout and real interaction checks remain unperformed. Existing double escaping and the unchanged remote-folder encoded-key lookup issue are documented rather than silently included in this security change. Server-side plugin URL validation and other templates remain outside scope.
